[[Inter-calls at the SPÖ.]] We have too many social security schemes, we have bad decision-making structures and too many bodies. I can list many things that show that we don't have this in Austria. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Scherak.]] The potential for savings is always questioned, so I quote: The savings potential in the administration of the Austrian social security scheme is 200 to 300 million euros per year through slimming structures and pooling, standardisation and automation of similar activities. - Quote. Good, so much for savings. For me and for us as a government, however, one important thing is: We're the insured person, the patient important, and he's at the center. What does that mean? – This means that there is still compulsory insurance, that the representatives of the insured continue to exist within the framework of self-management, and that there is, above all, justice in the system – and this is one of the most important principles at all – that means equal contributions, equal benefits. [[Congratulations with FPÖ and ÖVP.]] There will be no increase in contributions. Why? – Because we can save in the system and provide this rationalisation potential to the insured. That means we have sustainable funding. There will be no privatisation whose execution of the opposition will be so gladly given to the Pranger. We will not advance expropriation of the countries. We will promote a system of solidarity and modernity. Ladies and gentlemen, it is also very important for me to say: The structural reform of social security is the beginning of a health reform. Why is this necessary? – The last government has actually driven patients into the ambulances, because in the hospital sector this is a different pot. She drove her into the electoral system. Why? – You have to pay for yourself and get only 80 percent of the rate back. All these are points that the patient feels outside. We want to change that. We want more cash doctors and more domestic doctors, we don't want waiting times and a performance alignment with the stationary area. These are the challenges. We focus on the patient. [[Call at the SPÖ:Ah, that's why the list of shortcomings is extended!]] The subject of experts was of course discussed by several sides. [[Congratulations on members of FPÖ and ÖVP.]] This, ladies and gentlemen, is the trans-regional mediation by the AMS. The aim is to address the unemployed from the other Länder and to transfer them to the free workplaces. In this context, the rules on feasibility should be evaluated accordingly. In addition to an internal-Austrian mobility package, the Austrian Business Agency is also intended to recruit experts within the EU. Let me say one more to the teaching: Career with teaching has long been a keyword. The question is whether the model is still fit for the future. There are different genes, and the generation Z is, as you know, those who at the moment need to be motivated to go into teaching. That means we have to look: What motivates young people, what motivates the generation Z? – It is not especially the career, the money, but above all the meanings and, of course, a certain work-life balance. That is, as a government, we are also very much concerned with what the motivation of young people is for getting back or even into the work process. There are, as you know, regional imbalances in open teaching places, which concern the West, but also Vienna. In Vienna, for example, there are six times more job seekers than open apprenticeships. Not only are young people and their environment, parents and educators, but, of course, companies must also present themselves as attractive employers and offer further apprenticeships. I think that's a goal, it's a must that companies really get more involved. Of the 60 000 unemployed under 25 years of age, the training of skilled workers is also to be forced. Over-company training is intended to further improve access to occupational training by intensifying the share of practice, which is about 14 500 persons per year. I also think that the investment foundations are a very important instrument, which should also be extended accordingly. The AMS organises and finances this precisely according to the qualification requirements of the companies. At present, some 7,000 people are qualified for a concrete staff requirement within the framework of the placement foundations. In addition, in 2017, about 3 300 people and in the first half of 2018 about 1,800 people were included in the job-related qualification programme aimed at the occupation of individual jobs. Now on a subject on which the opposition insecurity was operated, namely the AMS budget. By the end of August 2018, the number of unemployed, as you know, fell by around 9 percent, the common number of trainees and unemployed, fell by about 8 percent. [[Inter-calls at the SPÖ.]] It is also particularly gratifying that the number of unemployed in the labour market has fallen. It is really very, very important to me to benefit from the good economic situation in 2019 and to put the focus of labour market policy on a rapid and early placement of people seeking employment. This requires, on the one hand, the expansion of automation and, on the other, personalised labour market care. After a focus has been placed on promoting refugees in recent years, I am particularly concerned about the integration of long-term unemployed, older unemployed, people with disabilities, women and young people. [[Congratulations with FPÖ and ÖVP.]] Of course, the promotion of generation 50 plus is of great importance. Ladies and gentlemen, with a funding budget of 1.251 billion euros, that is 3 494 euros per capita, the AMS has sufficient resources at its disposal. [[Congratulations with the FPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] 3 494 euros per head, which is 275 euros per head more than 2017. Participation is an important parameter in a society. Only a society that strives for inclusion and not only reduces the barriers, but rather does not make it come about at all, is one that meets modern social progress. Of course, the National Action Plan, which provides for 250 measures for 2012 to 2020, will not end in 2020, but will be continued, I can guarantee it now. For this reason, €84.4 million will be allocated to this area in the compensation tax fund in 2018. The aim must be for people with disabilities to enrich businesses to meet the modern social progress mentioned at the outset. Many positive measures have already been taken and many more will follow. Let me mention another aspect, namely increased family support. An important action that testifies to the Federal Government's intention to support people with disabilities with all its power is, of course, to ensure family support. The Administrative Court has decided in its recent case law that people whose livelihood is financed more than 50 percent by the state will not have a right to increased family support in the future. This increased family allowance for families with disabled children, however, is aimed at paying off the additional expenses to be incurred due to the disability of a child. I strongly support the support of people with disabilities and am therefore pleased that the government parties today will ensure through the revision of the FLAG that those affected will also have the right to increased family support in the future. Jarolim.]] On the matter: I agree with Mr Fürlinger and Mr Ragger that we really need to do everything we can to reduce the risk of terrorism, possibly to do something preventative, which reduces the risk of terrorist attacks. The border is there, where out of a perhaps a little populist or even hypertrophic need for punishment something is normed, which is absurd, and absurd is § 278g. “Whoever travels to another state to commit a criminal act” and so on and so on “to commit” – that is overwhelming, and it is not necessary either. Now, of course, I know Article 9 of the EU Anti-Terrorism Directive. Member States are required to punish travel. But this is just the case today! When you look at § 278g, you see that two possible cases, two imaginable cases for a criminal behavior according to this new paragraph are possible. Of course, the first case-constituency, which can be imagined below, is always in question only for individual perpetrators who travel abroad for the purpose of a crime of terrorist association, training for terrorist purposes or guidance to commit a terrorist crime. But now the Supreme Court – unfortunately this is not included in the explanations – has given the Minister – already judged – to the House at the latest with 14Os66/18i: If a person from Austria has contact with a terrorist association and promises to arrive for training, for example, he is already subject to criminal law. This is already punishable, and therefore there is no need here in the case of a travel commitment to introduce a new crime. We have a second case constellation, and this second case constellation occurs when this journey is envisaged to commit a terrorist crime according to § 278c. This is about injury to the body, damage to property and also the need. Here too, the Supreme Court has already said: Those who inform others of their intention to do something like this in Austria will already be penalised here in Austria in accordance with existing criminal law standards. There is no problem, there is no need to do anything. Notabene and footnote: Why is one of Austria allowed to travel abroad to kill someone if, for example, he decides to drive to Bavaria in Salzburg to commit a murder there? That's not punishable. But if there is somehow a suspicion of a terrorist act in the sense of physical injury or damage to property, he is now being punished for up to five years. In the review procedure, Professor Tipold made it quite clear that this is an unequal treatment, which I do not think is useful and not accessible. The last two points on this matter: We know that, in criminal law, it is of considerable legal and also preventive importance that we should be able to get out of the experimental stage, free from what we have expected. ==References====External links== But the joke at the formulation of this § 278g is not just that you don't know when it starts at all. Does that start when he calls the taxer to drive to Schwechat? Did he make himself punishable? Or does he make himself punishable until he checks in, or even when he's on the plane? When does the trip even begin? When is there even an end to the experiment or the beginning of the experiment? The far bigger problem is that someone who has already sat on the plane and made himself punishable by it has no reason at all to withdraw from the attempt to do so if he is looking for what he has expected in terms of damage to property or injury without having received a five-year penalty. Why, then, should he refrain from firing the bomb that he originally accepted? All in all: I think it's hypertrophic, I think it's criminal dogmatically wrong. If you believe that you have to go to the gold plating in this way – and I am now unfortunately coming to the favorite word of Mr. Minister – that is, something overcrowded, then that has a certain populist move, which I believe is not appropriate, particularly in the area of criminal law. We should refrain from § 278g. Thank you.

With regard to the implementation of the Anti-Terrorism Directive – the individual cases which are now being implemented – I would like to address a few points. Mrs Griss pointed out, among other things, that an extension of domestic jurisdiction would not be possible and that the perpetrator would have to be in Austria at the time of the act and not, as it is planned, be able to take place in Austria even if the act took place abroad and the perpetrator subsequently went to Austria, i.e. in the course of the course of his stay in Austria. You are right, it was originally the OGH who has held that for the punishment, domestic criminality, it is necessary that the perpetrator has at least an ordinary stay in Austria at the time of the act. However, there have been several OGH decisions in recent times, pointing out that it is sufficient to justify domestic jurisdiction if the perpetrator establishes his residence or normal residence after the time of the case in Austria and currently consists of a domestic residence or ordinary residence of the defendant. In other words, with the intention of extending domestic jurisdiction, we follow the case law of the OGH, i.e. we are not superfluous. We are resolving the judicial controversy and leading it in a direction that leads to legal certainty being created. Mr Noll, as well as in the Committee, expressed his concern about the extension of the catalogue of financial crimes. To this end, I would like to mention that in the future, in particular, the financing of training for terrorist purposes – that is, terrorist camps – and the travel there and also the financing of terrorism as such will be penalised. Similarly, the financing of a trip for terrorist purposes was addressed in the sense of a crime, you have also carried it out, that is the § 278g of the Criminal Code. [[towards Abg. Noll]] [[Intrusion of the Abg. Noll.]] In this context, they also stated that there would be a gold plating in this area. I can point out that, although you are right, it is not necessary, § 278d para. 1 in the framework of the Anti-Terrorism Directive, but on the other hand this is precisely the implementation of the Terrorism Directive, that at the same time we are implementing the UN Convention against Terrorism Financing, and it is precisely this Convention that requires that we also punish these journeys accordingly. In other words, we are not superfluous in this area, but we are also implementing the UN Convention against Terrorism Financing. As regards the introduction of the new crime for terrorist travel, you are right that in the event that several participate in a journey that has already been punishable. New is that a single criminal who travels alone is also being punished. In this context, you have also asked the question: When does the journey begin, when does this take place? . I would like to point out that the OGH has already seen the announcement to members of a terrorist organisation to travel to them and to actively participate as a physical contribution to action. So we also follow the case law of the OGH and thus implement the Terrorism Directive. Finally, Mr Jarolim and Mrs Bayer, in connection with the so-called Freedom Fighters, I would like to mention that I am grateful for the fact that opinions have been made and that they have been given the same position. It has been pointed out several times in the opinion procedure that the maintenance of the Freedom Fighters could very well be in line with the Terrorism Directive. However, I would also like to point out that we also complied with this because we asked Member States whether they had problems, we would continue to provide the Freedom Fighters as an exception. The fact that this originally happened – and I do not want to mention that – was because Austria at the time, in the negotiations of the Terrorism Directive, absolutely wanted the Freedom Fighters to remain upright, but the other Member States did not agree to it. As a result of these discussions in the various Council working groups, the Freedom Fighters were not foreseen in the original assessment draft. But in this context – you have referred to it – it shows: If one works together and at the same time points to problems, this can lead to a very positive result. I therefore thank you for this result and hope that, in the sense of greater legal certainty, including in the sense of combating terrorism, this proposal will be approved. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-09-26-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00039_d7e11110	Madam President. Mr. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Dear visitors to the gallery and in front of the screens at home! This is a change of the GmbH Act and the Notarial Order. The main focus is on two subjects. The first area is the creation of an electronic notarial act created in a video conference, so that not all the shareholders must be present at the same time at the Notary. The present draft law is another step that simplifies the creation of a GmbH using modern communication technologies. But at the same time – and this is an essential point – the high level of safety will be maintained by attracting notaries here. There was a test phase for the complete digital GmbH foundation. In this test phase, it was seen that the pursuits of the digitisation of society's law were fully in line both at the Austrian level on the one hand and at the European level on the other. At the same time, however, the expectations of the founders themselves have been met, with simplification, while maintaining the high process reliability of the entire founding process. For the establishment of a GmbH, a company contract must be concluded in the form of a notarial act. The notary shall carry out an identification of the parties and, within the scope of its capacity, also verify the ability to act. Furthermore, the notary has to exercise the obligation to teach the shareholders about the possibilities for the design of the document and also to point out liability risks to the shareholders. In addition, the Notar has far-reaching audit and care obligations, which are an essential element in ensuring legal certainty in this area. It is also associated with: It serves to prevent money laundering and terrorist financing, to prevent social and tax fraud and also to enforce consumer and creditor protection – also a very important point that will be forgotten in the debate. The difficulty of the simultaneous presence of all founders was identified as a cause for delay. We have had the experience that, in some cases, holidays, hospitals or occupational absences sometimes lead to delays in the start-up process. In this respect, this digital GmbH foundation is a possibility for further acceleration of the founding process. It is also a cost saving for shareholders who have to take on a further arrival. The second point in this draft law concerns legal clarifications as regards the scope and scope of the notarial obligations in the framework of the signature certificates. Here there is a clarification, but in some parts there is also an intensification. There is a small discrepancy between the ruling opinion in literature on the one hand, also in the Judicture, in particular that of the European Court of Justice, and on the other hand the text of the verbatim legislation. The notary is generally obliged to respect the law and, according to the literature, has to refuse a certification if a forbidden business is to be signed. The same applies also to fake and circumvention transactions, to transactions that have unlawful disadvantages to a third party. However, in order for the notary to be able to assess this at all, he must also look at the document at least in terms of content in the main guidelines. This is something which, in large parts, already corresponds to the notarial practice, and this is still clarified in the law. On the previous speeches by Mr Noll – he is now, I believe, not here in the hall anymore – and I would just like to say one thing to Jarolim: The current law is only an adaptation to modern technical possibilities without giving up legal certainty. So the notaries will do exactly what they have done so far in the future, only that they will be able to do so in a digital form, in the form of a video conference. There is no reason at all for a shift in professional images, as some of which have been mentioned here, between notaries and lawyers. The notary as a neutral great-kunds person is more important today than ever. However, it is not only about the liability risks of the shareholders, but also about the limitation of liability, which in turn entails risks for creditors and consumers. If I have a contractual partner today who restricts his liability, the risk will be shifted to others. So it surprises me a little bit that criticism comes from the SPÖ ranks here – at least you once wrote the consumer protection very much on your flags. It seems that social democracy has thrown some values out of the past and now also consumer protection overboard. [[Inter-calls at the SPÖ.]] In this sense, I am pleased that we are taking an important step towards a more modern company law today, but without having to abandon the quality and legal certainty of our SMEs and the economic location in Austria. Thank you.

The law describes cooperatives as “personal associations with legal personality of non-closed members, which essentially serve – and that is crucial – “the promotion of the acquisition or the economy of their members”. The idea and meaning of cooperatives is simply – very important! –: achieve more with each other. This has made Friedrich Wilhelm Raiffeisen and Hermann Schulze-Delitzsch the basic principle as a pioneer of cooperatives in the new, today's form: Where things are too big for the individual, many work together and agree on what an individual cannot do. Examples are – and this is certainly not the case now –: I can't sell vegetables alone. We join forces to form a sales association. Or: I am a farmer and I cannot afford a mower alone, so both the procurement and the use of this mower are organized jointly in the machine ring. Or: I do not have access to a credit, so I am or am participating in a bank, such as Raiffeisen and the Volksbankengruppe are extremely successful in and for Austria. Or: I have no apartment, so I join or establish a housing cooperative so that this cooperative will build the house for me. Even though this division law, I say it again, is definitely not applicable to the housing cooperatives, because we wanted to protect the principle of property commitment and ensure that this cannot be avoided, I still say: From the idea a housing cooperative is, of course, a cooperative. The fact is, the cooperatives are at the same time owners and users. The essence of the cooperative is characterized by the fact that one does not want to achieve a return at all costs, but rather that one wants to be profitable in the long run, but puts the benefits to the attention of the members. Therefore: We're closing this law gap today. I take note with great enthusiasm that the SPÖ now also agrees to this bill. I think that's good and right, because it makes sense. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the FPÖ.]] In the future, we can separate individual business areas of a cooperative, such as IT or purchasing, or we can also separate regional locations. It was important for us that there were still important points in the review. The rights of the auditor have been extended to protect creditors, and members who do not agree to a division have been granted the right to belong to one or the other cooperative. Ladies and gentlemen, cooperatives mean one for all, all for one. In this sense, it is extremely important for me and all of us to maintain, promote and modernise this legal form. In this sense, I think: The 1 700 cooperatives in Austria now have the opportunity to split up in order to be more efficient, better and innovative in the sense of the members. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-09-26-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00039_d7e11621	The next item is the speech by Mr Tschank. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-09-26-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00039_d7e11642	Madam President. Mr. Federal Minister! High house! Dear ladies and gentlemen at home in front of the screens! It's about creating a new law, and, as I mean, a very good law. The legal form of the cooperative has so far been the Legislators have been treated a little stepmotherly. I am very pleased that this will change a lot with the present cooperative division law. In contrast to capital companies – GmbHs, stock companies; my predecessors have already mentioned this – there is no way for cooperatives to achieve a division within the meaning of the right of re-establishment, which is a major disadvantage to capital companies. This gap, we want to eliminate this disadvantage here, because in Germany too, a division of cooperatives has long been possible. For the many spectators at home: What does splitting cooperatives mean? – A cooperative should be allowed to transfer its assets to a new or existing cooperative in whole or in part by means of the overall succession of rights. What's important about this? – A correct exchange ratio of shares. The shareholders of the transferred cooperative are to become proportionate shareholders of the new cooperative. An expert revisor is required to confirm a positive transport value of the new cooperatives, thus also considering the interests of members and creditors alike. The protection of creditors is particularly important, i.e. those who are interested in the fact that their liability funds are no longer depleted towards the transferred cooperative. All these important points are taken into account in the new law. It should be emphasized, ladies and gentlemen, that there is a balanced mechanism in the so-called non-proportional division, i.e. in a division where the shares in the new cooperative are not allocated in that relation as is the participation of the members in the transferive cooperative. What possibilities does a member of the cooperative have in this case if it is dissatisfied? – It has a right of termination – i.e. a member can cancel his membership of the transferred or new cooperative and will subsequently also be paid out – or a right of vote – that is, a member may demand that he or she participate in another cooperative. This means that from our point of view, the members of the cooperative have a very high level of action, and this is extremely positive, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. That is why the present proposal of a cooperative division law is all in all very good. This certainly increases the legal form of cooperatives quite, very much. We express our support. Thank you very much.

In the course of a division, assets of the company to be divided, which is also referred to as a transferred company, are transferred to other companies in such a way that the shareholders of the transferred company are involved in the acquiring company. Thus, the division of societies represents the counterpart to the merger. It makes sense, therefore, that there is a division in this respect, and very gratifying that in 2018 we will use the possibility of law enforcement. We have worked on this for three years and – once again – waited 22 years for us to finally complete this process. That is why I would like to thank the chairman of the Justice Committee Michaela Steinacker for his commitment to this law, to our Minister who has made it possible, as well as to the ladies and gentlemen in the Ministry and the experts in the Austrian cooperative association. I would also like to mention the Raiffeisen Association, and I thank the SPÖ for its approval. Thank you, ladies and gentlemen.

Point Report of the Justice Committee on the Government Template (113d.b._Other Organiser Declaration by the Republic Austria on the adoption of the accessions of Panama, Uruguay, Colombia and El Salvador to the Convention on Civil Aspects of International Child Abduction (265 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2018-09-26-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00039_d7e11891	We're now on the 5th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mrs Salzmann is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-09-26-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00039_d7e11919	Madam President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, Ladies and gentlemen at the visitor gallery and at home on televisions! ==References== The Hagueer, adopted in October 1980 Child Abduction Convention is a Convention on Civil Aspects of International Child Abduction. The Hague Convention is a multilateral treaty that seeks to protect children from the physical and physical damage and consequences of these cross-border abductions and withdrawals. These are cases in which a parent usually transfers to another country under 16 years of age or holds it there in that other country and does not return it. To illustrate the whole thing a little, let me give you a few examples: Imagine a Moroccan father who lives with his child in Austria and travels on vacation to Morocco. He no longer brings this child back, but brings it there with his Moroccan relatives, and one can no longer be held liable to the child. Back in Austria, a mother who is worried and desperate because she is not sure if she will see her child again and when she will see it again, and, of course, how this child is doing. I think every mother, every father can get into something like this. Or imagine an Austrian living with her husband in Greece. She travels with her child to Austria, almost on holiday home, and no longer goes back to Greece. This mother withdraws her child from her father. Or a third case, so that this also becomes quite plausible: A father no longer brings the child back to the mother, or perhaps no longer to the caretaker, after a visitation contact, which is strictly regulated, when we think of a youth welfare facility, where this child may be accommodated, but he brings it to his home country. All of this, ladies and gentlemen, is cases of child abductions, which do not occur every day in Austria, but which always pay attention worldwide. In doing so, children are separated from a parent against their will and very often integrated into a society that is often completely foreign to them. However, the deprived parent – forgive me if I call it so – the parent from whom the child has been deprived can often suffer very hard. Also, knowing or not knowing whether to see it continues leads to despair very often. Ladies and gentlemen, in my profession I have a lot to do with children. The child soul is actually the most vulnerable, which is possible and imaginable, and this child, this child soul must protect it. This is where society and here the rule of law cannot watch. It is important to protect and preserve this child, his soul and the growing up of this child, in familiar surroundings. International child abduction, ladies and gentlemen, is a socio-political problem in a today very plural and global society. The fact is that, in particular, binational partnerships and marriages, this danger is much more common in this risk. The return of children is also difficult because several legal systems are involved. It is precisely here that the Hague Child Abduction Convention begins and tries with the Contracting States to deal with an accelerated procedure with the Contracting Parties and to ensure that these children can be returned as quickly as possible. Until now, 98 Parties have signed this Hague Child Abduction Convention, 65 States have joined, 27 States have ratified. Since this HChu is, as it is short term, legislative amending and supplementing legislation, accession of new countries also requires the adoption by the contracting parties. That is now the point where we are getting into play in Parliament. As Member States, we need EU empowerment. This authorisation was issued by EU Council Decision in December 2017 and the Council of Ministers has already blessed in May 2018 the Declaration of Approval of Austria for the States of Panama, Uruguay, Colombia and El Salvador. Ladies and gentlemen! It is up to us to look at the welfare of these children and to avoid lasting harm. In this respect, I would also like to ask for the approval of this declaration of acceptance. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-09-26-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00039_d7e12038	Mrs Holzinger-Vogtenhuber is next spoken. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-09-26-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00039_d7e12059	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, Yes, the accession of Panama, Uruguay, Colombia and El Salvador to the Hague Convention on the Civil Aspects of International Child Abduction, on the one hand, naturally increases legal certainty in matters of competence and procedures, but I would like to point out that the original thrust of this 1980 agreement, i.e. protection in the event of actual child abduction, is no longer the case today. The most common cases in which it is applied are nowadays cases of concern. In most cases, it is about a parent coming from a bina- national marriage – if, for example, an Austrian marries a Colombian – usually the mother, with whom the common child cannot simply return to her home country of Austria after separation or divorce. To construct a case: An Austrian who married a Colombian and has two children with him, de facto cannot take her children to Austria if the father simply does not want to do so. In Colombia, the common care is automatically applied in divorce, and, of course, under the agreement, the man in Colombia is pressing the Hague Agreement button in non-unification, and thus the child is immediately flagged and brought back. I believe that this is a very serious issue, but very little about the child welfare itself. This is also my approach and my appeal to the Federal Government: The child's well-being must be at the centre of this place and even stronger into the center. That is why my appeal, in the revision of the Hague Agreement, is to put the child's well-being at the centre when it is really necessary to revise the Hague Agreement. I would also like to ask you: Take this and rework it! – Thank you very much.

Why not? – They have spent everything from the money they took. What we just don't need is the mentality that you spend the money you have, because you seem to have it right now, simply because it's there. A formative financial policy must make use of opportunities for investment and discharges. Now it would be possible, for example, if one were to make a forward-looking economy, to say: Hey, we're clearing old debts. That would be an investment in the future. But that is not really the case in what we discussed in the spring budget. This is at the expense of the following generations, this is the famous backpack that we are about to hug our children and grandchildren. Forming financial policy, education, research, development, economic location – this is important that has been made a part of it. We need citizens' discharges. We need a reduction in government debt to minimise the risk of rising interest rates. The Federal Government has undertaken to create a zero deficit for 2019. This is not very ambitious in view of the continuing economic growth. What I would like to say: It simply takes this effort to get a little deeper in and clean up the budget. Our concepts are on the table. Mr. State Secretary, you know her. You will not all agree to one thing, but you have the duty to use the situation and, above all, the economic situation now. Here I would like to give you the following: Have courage! Have the courage to go where it hurts! You've announced a lot. You've announced good things. Now we are dealing with the implementation and enforcement of these announcements. Just not to make new debts – as Mr Angerer said – will simply not be enough at the moment. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-09-26-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00039_d7e12725	Madam President. High house! I do not want to use my speaking time today to address details of the financial statements, but I would like to use my speaking time to initiate a reform of the financial statements and the debate on them in plenary. When we discuss the budget, we are always discussing this in committee and in plenary, with the presence of all ministers. When we examine the accounts, we usually discuss this in the Committee on Budgets, with the presence of the President of the Court of Auditors and in the plenary session, only in the presence of the President of the Court of Auditors. Thankfully, Mr Fuchs, the State Secretary is sitting there today, but it is not usually. And why isn't that usual? . This is not usual, therefore, because the financial statements are formally only discussed as a report by the Court of Auditors in plenary. To be honest, I am very sorry because the budget implementation is something that should be discussed here in plenary, but also in the Committee, because it is still a matter of checking whether a government has implemented the objectives it has set itself in the budget. I've always wondered why. The explanation is relatively simple: In contrast to other countries, in Austria the drawing up of accounts and auditing of accounts are institutionally in one hand, in the hand of the Court of Auditors. I believe that in the private sector it would be unthinkable that the drawing up of the accounts and the subsequent review are in one hand. This should, of course, also be different with regard to the federal accounts. In the majority of states, it is true that two years ago, a study that I have commissioned in the budget service, has shown, that the responsibilities for drawing up and auditing the accounts are clearly separate and therefore, of course, transparency is quite different from that of us. But since this is in one hand with us in Austria, it is also so strange that we have a debate on this conclusion here, in which not all the members of the government sit on the government bank, but usually the President of the Court of Auditors. Of course, I would also like to discuss this conclusion with you, but I would rather do so with you and the entire government. [[in the direction of President of the Court of Auditors Kraker]] If I say now that the Court of Auditors is preparing this document, I am saying at the same time that this is done, of course, with the help of the Ministry of Finance and the budgetary authorities. That is, they already do most of the pre-work anyway. What else does the Court of Auditors do? – The Court of Auditors then examines this clearance of accounts in the so-called failure settlement procedure in accordance with § 9 of the Court of Auditors Act. But since the responsibilities are not very clear, it is also true in the present system that a statement of confirmation by the Court of Auditors would be unthinkable. Since the Court of Auditors is obliged to correct the error-solving procedure, it is very strongly responsible for the accuracy of the accounts, but for which it is actually responsible. This is an absurdity we have in Austria. As a result of this study, which I have assigned to the budget service, we once had a debate on it in a supplementary budget advisory board. The result was that a working group under the direction of the Ministry of Finance and the Court of Auditors was set up, which should discuss it. Perhaps in this legislative period we will succeed in eliminating this Austrian unicum. We may also be able – and we are all here in the National Council – to come up with a meaningful debate on how the objectives can be examined in the financial statements and whether they are in line with what was included in the federal proposal. Finally, I have the following request to you, Madam President of the Court of Auditors: Can you tell us something about the state of affairs in this working group? Thank you.

Thomas Drozda, colleagues on the failure of confidence towards the Federal Minister for the Interior In the course of the debate on the urgent question concerning ‘Frontal attack by the Ministry of the Interior on freedom of the press’ Reasons ==References== On September 9, 2018, it was announced that the Federal Ministry of the Interior has sent an e-mail containing recommendations for action to all the national police departments. In the letter, the spokesman of the Ministry of the Interior points out that “certain media”, such as the weekly newspaper “Falter” and the daily newspapers “Der Standard” and “Kurier”, “very one-sided and negative reporting about the BMI or the BMI. the police. These “critical media” would simply ignore facts and explanations. Furthermore, the e-mail states that “communication with these media should be limited to the most necessary (legally provided) measure”. They should not be allowed to “sugarl, such as exclusive accompaniment” unless “neutral or even positive reporting” is expected. These recommendations of action in the letter of the Ministry of the Interior are a forward-tal attack on freedom of the press. If critical media are deprived of information and background information or exclusive reports are only to be provided with uncritical or well-satisfied media, this is a violation of constitutionally guaranteed freedom of the media. If some media prefer access to information, while others are consciously disadvantaged, this also violates the principle of equality. Freedom of the media and the press and that contained the right of the media and the public to access information is a cornerstone of our liberal democracy. In order to ensure the diversity of the media and the right of citizens to a pluralistic supply of information, it is essential that every_r Jour-nalist_in has the same opportunities to access information from state institutions, and the public authorities of their duty to provide the media with comprehensive information about the unscrupulous differentiation. In addition, the letter from the Ministry of the Interior also suggested that in future police broadcasts generally call citizenship and the residence status of suspects and proactively communicate sexual offences, provided that they are not a family act. So far, victim protection has been to prevent the victim's traumatisation through wide-ranging reporting. So far, according to a decree from the Ministry of Justice, a mention of the perpetrator’s origin should only be made if “this is absolutely necessary for the understanding of the process reported. ” Why these principles should be dealt with is not understandable. An interior minister who deliberately wants to control the focus of reporting in order to stir up resentments and split society with his propaganda is an unbearable security risk for Austria. Such a letter should never have been sent by the Secretary-General of the Ministry of the Interior. A minister who under his leadership allows such an attack on freedom of the press is no longer viable. After the publication of the letter to the public, although the BMI tried to dispute in a broadcast, the letter had no binding character, it again stressed the bias of certain media. It was only about 24 hours later that Herbert Kicklselbst distanced himself in an issue half-heartedly from some of the formulations chosen in the letter of his representative speaker. The Minister of the Interior, however, rejected both the public and the recipients of the letter and all other departments bound by his instructions to correct the problematic points of the letter without delay. The damage to freedom of the press has already been done. Once again it has been shown that Minister of the Interior Herbert Kickleine is a danger to liberal democracy in Austria. ParlaMint-AT_2018-09-26-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00039_d7e17821	Mr President. Federal Minister! Mrs. State Secretary! Mr State Secretary! High house! Appreciate fellow citizens! We are dealing with the subject of freedom of the press today. Let me briefly highlight the basics and the reasons why freedom of the press is so important. We have a basis in Article 10 of the ECHR and we have a fundamental right to the Article 13 of the Basic Law, in which freedom of expression is fundamentally protected as constitutionally guaranteed law. This freedom of expression is a bearing pillar of our state, our democracy and society, and it includes not only the freedom to express its opinion but also the freedom, information and opinions to express freely. Press freedom guarantees that citizens can also exercise these freedoms effectively. We must protect and defend these rights. Fundamental rights mean not only freedoms in the state, but also freedoms of the citizens of the state. In this sense, our Federal Chancellor Sebastian Kurz yesterday clearly held the following very clearly – I may quote –: ‘The exclusion or boycott of selected media must not take place in Austria. This shall apply to all ministries and public bodies responsible for communication.’ "For free and independent journalism in the country, parties and government institutions and public institutions in particular have a high level of responsibility. Any restriction on freedom of the press is unacceptable.” In principle, I should like to continue: Our constitution has three state powers: the executives, that is, the ministries and the administrative bodies of our Republic, the legislative bodies, we are here as legislators in Parliament and in the country days, and the judicial bodies of the case law, i.e. the courts. Let me now highlight the special role of the media: Media are often referred to as the fourth violence in the state, and I believe they fulfil an indispensable task in democracy. They are the ones that give room to public opinion, and they are a public watchdog that controls state action. They can very significantly influence political events in our country, so they are particularly responsible for objective reporting and information. Journalists are required to fulfil this responsibility as well. We are living from pluralism in democracy, pluralism in reporting and media access to information, and it is therefore quite clear that the preference of individual media and the censorship or restriction of others is to be rejected, above all by state institutions. Above all, public institutions and people in the public sector must in principle be criticised. I think that's where we all agree: The Ministry's e-mail should not have been written. However, the Federal Minister made it clear yesterday that he did not know this e-mail, that he was against a restriction on freedom of the press, and that the directives in this respect were immediately revised. That's where we take him by the word, he's also responsible for us accordingly. However, I would like to come to another point: Police information can be very sensitive. Just as the Ministry of the Interior bears a special responsibility in the issue of information, the media bear the responsibility not to insure the population by unilateral reporting or distorted presentation or to incite fears. The sentence, the presumption of innocence, must by no means be a mere empty phrase. We live in a secure rule of law, our police and our justice do excellent work, but even if someone has committed a crime and the conditions for a condemnation are not of origin or skin color, religion, sexual orientation, political or other world view. There must be no division into good and bad offenders. One thing is clear to me indisputably: This e-mail, which was written – the author has now seen his fault – certainly cannot shake the foundations of our democracy. [[President Bures shall take over the chairmanship.]] If Mrs Belakovich had to correct several times before, Mr Pilz – quite right what you said! – then I can only say to everyone in an appeal: We are lucky to live in a healthy pluralist democracy in which freedom of expression is a great asset. That is why I am looking for you all – here in the House, but also and above all the fellow citizens –: We have the opportunity to obtain a wide range of information, to form an opinion based on facts and not to take everything for a fair coin, nor to make statements by Members who say what they want under the protection of immunity. Thank you.

Point Report of the Court of Auditors of the Court of Auditors on the report of the Court of Auditors on Federal Presidential Election 2016 (relocation of the second election) – BUND 2018/43 series (III-179/277 d.B.)
ParlaMint-AT_2018-09-26-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00039_d7e23591	We're now on the 9th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. I call Mr Gahr. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-09-26-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00039_d7e23618	Madam President. Madam President of the Court of Auditors! Mrs. State Secretary! Dear ladies and gentlemen! High house! In the Federal Presidential election 2016, there were panels, especially in connection with the inadequate electoral cards, but also in the election process. The postponement of the re-election of the second election of the Federal Presidential election in 2016 is probably still well remembered to all of us. It was not for nothing that the Federal President's election re-election was declared the word of the year 2016. Thankfully, Madam President, the Court of Auditors has dealt with these problems very quickly and intensively, especially as regards the electoral cards. The examination took place from April to July 2017 and was conducted with the Ministry of the Interior. Four areas were examined in particular and examined: firstly, the task of the Ministry with regard to the procurement and provision of electoral pressure sites; secondly, the support of the electoral authorities in the performance of the election; thirdly, crisis management after notification of inadequate electoral cards; and fourthly, the preparations for the introduction of a Central Electoral Register. Overall, the Court of Auditors did not give a bad witness to the Ministry of the Interior, but it strongly pointed out the need for quality management and quality assurance. There have already been problems with constituency in 2010, then some things were missing in the bonding process. It's hard. The Court of Auditors therefore strongly advises in its recommendations that specific tasks and requirements should be defined and quality criteria formulated in the future tendering process. Overall, the postponement of the election has caused costs of 5.2 million euros and therefore the total cost of the Federal Presidential election has been increased by 8 percent. The printing company made a voluntary replacement contribution of EUR 500 000. The Court of Auditors has also noted that there must be improvements in the electoral cards, including the deadlines. There have been very short periods of time, and it was not possible for foreign Austrians to issue their electoral cards in good time, they have arrived late. In this respect, the Court of Auditors proposes legal changes, in particular for the storage of electoral acts and documents. A thorn in the eye of the Court of Auditors was, of course, also the compensation of the constituencies. 87 municipalities were surveyed, and only a small minority paid the fees according to the law on fees. Some municipalities also paid a little more than planned. The municipalities were created by the postponement of the second election of the Federal Presidential election cost of EUR 4.89 million, but they were largely covered by the federal government with approximately EUR 4.03 million. Ladies and gentlemen! Positively, the Court of Auditors assessed the measures to support local authorities and electoral authorities. Thus, the Ministry of the Interior set up an e-learning platform and developed a guide, which is to be continued, adapted to the respective choice. In the future, you should also remember a central register of voters. Our Secretary of State has spoken and answered the Committee and has been able to report positively that the Court of Auditors' recommendations have been fully implemented in the field of internal affairs. Finally, you can say: You've learned from mistakes. The best proof of this was that there was no problem in the National Council election last year and that it went over the stage flawlessly. Thank you very much for this report.

There is Europe-wide cooperation between the Federal Criminal Office and police units, in particular with the Balkans. In particular, it deals with the exchange of information on the fight against organised crime, in particular drug trafficking, human trafficking, financial crime and terrorism. However, in order to achieve this exchange, computer systems must be compatible so that data collected and processed can also be exchanged between countries. For example, if a suspect's DNA data is secured and retrieveable in a database, this is an enormous detection progress. Another focus is on cross-border cooperation in the field of illegal migration. Therefore, an international joint investigation office was established in the Austrian Federal Criminal Office. This investigative agency deals with the fight against tow and trafficking in human beings on the Balkan route. Here too, a rapid criminal police exchange of information in close cooperation with Europol will be ensured. At this point, I would like to thank all Austrian police officers, whether they are working abroad or in the country, for a great deal of thanks. There was the industrial revolution, mass production, the electricity has prevailed, the communication of any kind, the transport, the whole world has changed, Austria is no longer a purely agricultural state. What has not changed is petition law. It is the same as in 1867 and says – from the human image of that time it was also a revolution, today it is not so much – that citizens in this country have the opportunity to be heard; only to be heard, that is not to say, that they are understood. This does not mean that a dialogue is taking place. This does not mean that one encounters each other at eye level or, what in the 21st century. In the 19th century, a self-conception would be that one should bind them in and make a piece of far-reaching decisions comprehensible. ParlaMint-AT_2018-09-26-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00039_d7e26737	The next item is the speech by Mr Pewny. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-09-26-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00039_d7e26758	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Mr Bißmann, unfortunately you did not comply with my invitation, which I gave in June, to a joint observation drive in my company, otherwise you would already know why, from my point of view, the lifting of speed limitation is not a good idea. I will try to explain my point of view and explain why we need the 10-km/h speed limit on cyclist crossings. Cyclist crossings always have to be considered to be seen in order to be able to claim priority as cyclists. In particular, in connection with right-wing vehicles, a partial lifting of § 68 para. 3a more than dangerous. The handlebar can not detect the cyclist approaching at too high speed with the perfect mirror mirror-spiegel-Schulter-Blick in time. The dramatic consequence would be a traffic accident, in the worst case even with death. I can also quote the transport expert Nosé from the Mobility Club: “That’s why you should slowly approach and look for contact with the vehicle driver, because the slower you drive, the sooner you can be noticed.” You mean, Mrs Bißmann, that we can simply rely on reason and do not need this pass in the law, or that this would be even anti-terrorist. Unfortunately, reality shows us exactly the opposite. Ladies and gentlemen, since 2013, the number of bicycle accidents has increased by 18 percent. 4.7 percent of all road accidents occur on bicycle crossings. 6 percent of fatal bicycle accidents are due to too high a speed of the cyclist. Furthermore, ÖAMTC has recently carried out measurements on a bicycle crossing in Vienna. There 338 cyclists were measured and 89 percent of them were too fast. An average speed of 19 km/h was determined, some of which were even more than 30 km/h fast. We also know from surveys that half of the cyclists do not even know this 10-km/h rule. Not only from my point of view, my much-honored Ladies and gentlemen, but also from the point of view of the Mobility Club, it is therefore essential to comply with the applicable transport rules. Rather than delete this passage in the law, it would be important to create awareness of this important law. Mrs. Bißmann, my invitation is still standing. Come into my company and we'll make a joint observation journey! Thank you.
